@staticmethod\n    def exit_code(\n        equals: Any = _SENTINEL,\n        in_set: Optional[Iterable[int]] = None,\n        not_equals: Optional[int] = None,\n    ) -> ExpectCallable:\n        \"\"\"Assert the subprocess exit code (COMP-010.4).\n\n        Default behaviour asserts ``equals=0``. ``equals`` and ``in_set``\n        are mutually exclusive — supplying both raises ``ValueError`` at\n        primitive construction time. ``not_equals`` may be combined with\n        either.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # Detect explicit ``equals=`` so the default value (0) does not\n        # trip explicit_equals else 0\n\n        if explicit_equals and in_set is not None:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"Expect.exit_code: 'equals' and 'in_set' are mutually exclusive\"\n    def _build() -> tuple[bool, str, Mapping[str, Any], Optional[ExpectFailure]]:\n                code = ctx.exit_code\n\n                if normalized_set is not None:\n                    if code                ctx,\n                            name=\"exit_code\",\n                            expected={\"in_set\": sorted(normalized_set)},\n                            actual=code,\n The returned callable\n20\thas type `ExpectCallable = Callable[[EvalContext], ExpectResult]` (DM-009).\n21\t\n22\tThe default for `equals` is the module-level sentinel `_SENTINEL` (not\n23\t`0`) so the primitive can distinguish three states at construction time:\n24\t\n25\t| State | Detection | Behaviour |\n26\t|---|---|---|\n27\t| Implicit equals (no args, default applies) | `equals is _SENTINEL`, `in_set is None` | `effective_equals = 0` |\n28\t| Explicit equals | `equals is not _SENTINEL` | `effective_equals = equals`; mutex with `in_set` |\n29\t| `in_set` only | `in_set is not None`, `equals is _SENTINEL` | `in_set` wins; no equals check |\n30\t\n31\tThis sentinel-aware default is what makes\n32\t`Expect.exit_code(in_set={0, 1})` legal: a naive `equals=0` default\n33\twould trip the mutual-exclusion guard on every `in_set` call.\n34\t\n35\t## Argument semantics\n36\t\n37\t| Arg | Type | When set | Pass condition |\n38\t|---|---|---|---|\n39\t| `equals` | `int` (sentinel default) | Strict equality | `ctx.exit_code == equals`. Default `equals=0` applies only when neither `equals` nor `in_set` is supplied. |\n40\t| `in_set` | `Iterable[int]` | Membership | `ctx.exit_code` is a member of the normalised `frozenset(in_set)`. Accepts `set`, `list`, `tuple`, or any other iterable of ints. |\n41\t| `not_equals` | `Optional[int]` | Negative equality | `ctx.exit_code != not_equals`. Composable with either `equals` or `in_set`. |\n42\t\n43\t## Mutual-exclusion guard\n44\t\n45\t`equals` and `in_set` are mutually exclusive *only when `equals` is set\n46\texplicitly*. Else if `effective_equals is not None` and `code != effective_equals`:\n64\t   - FAIL with `expected=effective_equals`, `actual=code`,\n65\t     message `f\"exit_code expected {effective_equals} got {code}\"`.\n66\t3. This avoids the trap\n95\twhere a manifest mistakenly emits an empty list and silently passes.\n96\t`test_in_set_empty_iterable_always_fails` pins it.\n97\t\n98\t## Test matrix {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01F4Y6mGrFuLasQXsA4FQjEe","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01C9jX1X7QMyfEvtoa2HkhHD","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0069/spec.md","content":"# D-0069 — `Expect.stderr` / `Expect.stdout` primitive spec (COMP-010.5)\n\n**Task:** T04.07 (Phase 4, Roadmap R-069 / COMP-010.5)\n**Module:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py` — `Expect.stderr`, `Expect.stdout`\n**Tests:** `tests/cli/eval/test_expect_stdio.py` (23 cases)\n**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n\n## Signatures\n\n```python\nExpect.stderr(\n    contains: Optional[str] = None,\n    regex: Optional[str] = None,\n    not_contains: Optional[str] = None,\n) -> ExpectCallable\n\nExpect.stdout(\n    contains: Optional[str] = None,\n    regex: Optional[str] = None,\n    not_contains: Optional[str] = None,\n) -> ExpectCallable\n```\n\nBoth primitives share the same internal predicate engine\n(`_stream_predicate` in `expect.py`); only the buffer source differs.\n`Expect.stderr` reads from `ctx.stderr`; `Expect.stdout` reads from\n`ctx.stdout`. The returned callables have type\n`ExpectCallable = Callable[[EvalContext], ExpectResult]` (DM-009) and\ncarry `__name__` equal to `\"stderr\"` / `\"stdout\"` so the runner's JSONL\nlog records the originating primitive.\n\n## ANSI-strip dependency (COMP-011 / T02.17)\n\nThe buffer source is the **ANSI-stripped** transcript fed by\n`PtyStream` (T02.17 / D-0037). `PtyStream` strips CSI / OSC / DCS /\nSOS / PM / APC bursts before assembling line-buffered output, so by the\ntime the engine sees `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` the buffer is plain\ntext. The predicate engine then **re-strips ANSI** via\n`_strip_ansi(text)` → `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", text)` so manifests that\nload raw transcript fixtures still get clean substring / regex matches.\n\nThis double-strip is intentional. `PtyStream` is the on-the-wire\nproducer; the re-strip in the predicate engine guards against:\n\n* Fixture transcripts captured by hand (no PTY in the loop).\n* Future producers that feed `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` from a non-\n  PtyStream path.\n* Test authors who paste literal colour escapes into a regression\n  pinning a bug that previously leaked through.\n\n`test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match`,\n`test_stderr_strips_ansi_osc_before_match`, and\n`test_stdout_not_contains_after_ansi_strip` pin this contract.\n\n## Argument semantics\n\n| Arg | Type | Pass condition |\n|---|---|---|\n| `contains` | `Optional[str]` | `contains in buf` after ANSI-strip. |\n| `regex` | `Optional[str]` | `re.compile(regex).search(buf)` is not `None` after ANSI-strip. |\n| `not_contains` | `Optional[str]` | `not_contains not in buf` after ANSI-strip. |\n\n`None` means \"no check for this axis\". All three default to `None`; a\nno-argument invocation trivially passes (records the buffer length only).\n`test_stdout_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` and\n`test_stderr_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` pin the no-op contract.\n\n## Evaluation order\n\nInside the predicate engine, given `buf = _strip_ansi(source(ctx))`:\n\n1. If `contains is not None` and `contains not in buf` → FAIL with\n   `expected={\"contains\": contains}`, `actual=buf`, message\n   `f\"{name} missing substring {contains!r}\"`.\n2. Else if `regex is not None` and `compiled.search(buf) is None` →\n   FAIL with `expected={\"regex\": regex}`, `actual=buf`, message\n   `f\"{name} did not match regex {regex!r}\"`.\n3. Else if `not_contains is not None` and `not_contains in buf` →\n   FAIL with `expected={\"not_contains\": not_contains}`, `actual=buf`,\n   message `f\"{name} unexpectedly contained {not_contains!r}\"`.\n4. Otherwise PASS with `details={\"stream\": name}`, message\n   `f\"{name} predicates satisfied\"`.\n\nThe first failing check produces the `ExpectResult.failure` (DM-005);\n`test_stdout_contains_runs_before_regex` pins the ordering.\n`test_stdout_all_three_predicates_pass_together` covers the\nall-pass composition path.\n\n## Failure payload\n\n| Branch | `expected` | `actual` | Message |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `contains` miss | `{\"contains\": str}` | `str` (full buffer) | `{name} missing substring {contains!r}` |\n| `regex` miss | `{\"regex\": str}` | `str` (full buffer) | `{name} did not match regex {regex!r}` |\n| `not_contains` violation | `{\"not_contains\": str}` | `str` (full buffer) | `{name} unexpectedly contained {not_contains!r}` |\n\n`name` is the originating primitive (`\"stdout\"` or `\"stderr\"`).\n`actual` is the full ANSI-stripped buffer so the Reporter (T03.13)\ncan render context around the miss without re-fetching the artifact.\n\n## Test matrix (`tests/cli/eval/test_expect_stdio.py`)\n\n| # | Test | Stream | Argument under test | Direction |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `test_stdout_contains_passes_when_substring_present` | stdout | `contains` | PASS |\n| 2 | `test_stdout_contains_fails_when_substring_absent` | stdout | `contains` | FAIL |\n| 3 | `not_contains` | PASS |\n| 6 | `test_stdout_not_contains_fails_when_present` | stdout | `not_contains` | FAIL |\n| 7 | `test_stderr_contains_passes_when_substring_present` | stderr | `contains` | PASS |\n| 8 | `test_stderr_contains_fails_when_substring_absent` | stderr | `contains` | FAIL |\n| 9 | `test_stderr_regex_passes_on_match` | stderr | `regex` | PASS |\n| 10 | `test_stderr_not_contains_fails_when_present` | stderr | `not_contains` | FAIL |\n| 13 | `test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match` | stdout | ANSI CSI strip | PASS + FAIL |\n| 14 | The returned callables have type\n`ExpectCallable = Callable[[EvalContext], ExpectResult]` (DM-009) and\ncarry `__name__` equal to `\"stderr\"` / `\"stdout\"` so the runner's JSONL\nlog records the originating primitive.\n\n## ANSI-strip dependency (COMP-011 / T02.17)\n\nThe buffer source is the **ANSI-stripped** transcript fed by\n`PtyStream` (T02.17 / D-0037). `PtyStream` strips CSI / OSC / DCS /\nSOS / PM / APC bursts before assembling line-buffered output, so by the\ntime the engine sees `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` the buffer is plain\ntext. The predicate engine then **re-strips ANSI** via\n`_strip_ansi(text)` → `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE.sub(\"\", text)` so manifests that\nload raw transcript fixtures still get clean substring / regex matches.\n\nThis double-strip is intentional. `PtyStream` is the on-the-wire\nproducer; the re-strip in the predicate engine guards against:\n\n* Fixture transcripts captured by hand (no PTY in the loop).\n* Future producers that feed `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` from a non-\n  PtyStream path.\n* Test authors who paste literal colour escapes into a regression\n  pinning a bug that previously leaked through.\n\n`test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match`,\n`test_stderr_strips_ansi_osc_before_match`, and\n`test_stdout_not_contains_after_ansi_strip` pin this contract.\n\n## Argument semantics\n\n| Arg | Type | Pass condition |\n|---|---|---|\n| `contains` | `Optional[str]` | `contains in buf` after ANSI-strip. |\n| `regex` | `Optional[str]` | `re.compile(regex).search(buf)` is not `None` after ANSI-strip. |\n| `not_contains` | `Optional[str]` | `not_contains not in buf` after ANSI-strip. |\n\n`None` means \"no check for this axis\". All three default to `None`; a\nno-argument invocation trivially passes (records the buffer length only).\n`test_stdout_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` and\n`test_stderr_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` pin the no-op contract.\n\n## Evaluation order\n\nInside the predicate engine, given `buf = _strip_ansi(source(ctx))`:\n\n1. If `contains is not None` and `contains not in buf` → FAIL with\n   `expected={\"contains\": contains}`, `actual=buf`, message\n   `f\"{name} missing substring {contains!r}\"`.\n2. Else if `regex is not None` and `compiled.search(buf) is None` →\n   FAIL with `expected={\"regex\": regex}`, `actual=buf`, message\n   `f\"{name} did not match regex {regex!r}\"`.\n3. Else if `not_contains is not None` and `not_contains in buf` →\n   FAIL with `expected={\"not_contains\": not_contains}`, `actual=buf`,\n   message `f\"{name} unexpectedly contained {not_contains!r}\"`.\n4. Otherwise PASS with `details={\"stream\": name}`, message\n   `f\"{name} predicates satisfied\"`.\n\nThe first failing check produces the `ExpectResult.failure` (DM-005);\n`test_stdout_contains_runs_before_regex` pins the ordering.\n`test_stdout_all_three_predicates_pass_together` covers the\nall-pass composition path.\n\n## Failure payload\n\n| Branch | {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01NN9RvMUgQbyjGd1uxa6P22","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01XwFueJ6HAUHPXuAHJcFy5L","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0069/notes.md","content":"# D-0069 — Implementation notes\n\n## Why this task was test-only\n\nT04.01 (D-0064) shipped the full `Expect.stderr` / `Expect.stdout`\nbodies alongside the package skeleton. Both primitives already\nsupported:\n\n* Three named arguments: `contains`, `regex`, `not_contains` (each\n  `Optional[str]`, default `None`).\n* A shared `_stream_predicate` engine so any regression in one surfaces\n  on both.\n* Defensive ANSI re-strip via `_strip_ansi` → `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` so raw\n  transcript fixtures match clean.\n* Failure payloads with `expected` / `actual` (DM-005) and a\n  primitive-tagged callable name for JSONL logging.\n\nT04.07's deliverable is the **per-primitive acceptance harness** that\ntouches every named argument and the ANSI-strip invariant on both\nstreams. No body changes were required to\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py`.\n\n## Decisions\n\n1. **Double-strip ANSI on the predicate side, even though PtyStream\n   already strips upstream.** PtyStream is the on-the-wire producer,\n   but `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` are typed `str` and could in theory\n   be sourced from a non-PtyStream path in the future (manifest-loaded\n   transcript fixtures, test-injected buffers, replay tooling). The\n   defensive re-strip costs one regex `sub` per assertion and removes\n   an entire failure mode where a hidden `\\x1B[0m` would silently\n   break a substring check. `test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match`\n   pins both the match side (PASS) and the leak side (FAIL on a\n   literal escape) so a future producer that *fails* to strip can't\n   regress the contract.\n\n2. **Evaluation order: `contains` → `regex` → `not_contains`.** When\n   multiple predicates are supplied, the first failing check produces\n   the failure payload — the rest short-circuit. This matches the\n   declaration order in the signature so manifest authors reading\n   `Expect.stdout(contains=..., regex=..., not_contains=...)` see the\n   same priority the runtime applies.\n   `test_stdout_contains_runs_before_regex` pins it.\n\n3. **No-args is a degenerate PASS.** `Expect.stdout()` with no\n   predicates returns `passed=True` rather than raising a\n   \"no-predicate\" error at construction time. Rationale: manifest\n   authors sometimes want a sentinel assertion that the stream is\n   *reachable* (the EvalContext was populated) without making any\n   content claim. A construction-time raise would force them to invent\n   a no-op regex (`r\".\"` or similar) just to satisfy the API.\n   `test_stdout_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` and\n   `test_stderr_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` pin the no-op contract.\n\n4. **`actual` field carries the *full* stripped buffer, not a truncation.**\n   The Reporter (T03.13) renders unified diffs over the buffer when\n   building the failure section; truncation at the predicate layer\n   would force the Reporter to either re-fetch the artifact or emit\n   incomplete context. Memory is bounded by the per-eval log cap\n   enforced upstream by the orchestrator (T03.15), so handing the full\n   buffer down is safe.\n\n5. **Symmetric test matrix across the two primitives.** Both streams\n   get all 11 argument-coverage cases (contains × pass/fail, regex ×\n   pass/fail, not_contains × pass/fail, plus the ANSI-strip variants\n   on both, plus the envelope + declarative checks). Originally I\n   considered parametrizing across stream names to halve the file, but\n   the asymmetric ANSI variants (CSI on stdout, OSC on stderr) and the\n   distinct fixture inputs read more clearly as named tests. A future\n   change that adds a 4th argument can drop into the same matrix\n   without disturbing the existing layout.\n\n## What was NOT changed in src/\n\n* `Expect.stderr` and `Expect.stdout` bodies in\n  `src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py` were already complete after\n  T04.01. The T04.07 acceptance suite is purely additive.\n* `_stream_predicate`, `_strip_ansi`, `_named_callable`, and\n  `_timed_result` helpers were untouched.\n\n## Followups deferred\n\n* Test that `Expect.stdout` and `Expect.stderr` share golden output\n  shape when a single eval emits to both streams (planned for the\n  TEST-007 reporter contract suite, T04.17).\n* Performance benchmark on >1 MB ANSI-laden buffers (planned alongside\n  the disk-budget regression suite, T03.19).\n* Manifest-loader smoke test pairing `Expect.from_mapping` with a\n  fully assembled `expects:` block (planned alongside the manifest\n  loader landing in T05.x).\n\n## Linked artifacts\n\n* Spec: `D-0069/spec.md`\n* Evidence: `evidence/T04.07/pytest-output.txt`\n* Source: `src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py` (`Expect.stderr`,\n  `Expect.stdout`, `_stream_predicate`)\n* Test module: `tests/cli/eval/test_expect_stdio.py`\n* Roadmap: R-069, depends on T04.01 (D-0064), T02.17 (D-0037)\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3720,"cache_read_input_tokens":133694,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3720,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"681c02ed-0223-49a1-a62e-3a8e25e6ebb0","uuid":"08f7c591-64f5-472e-b948-55a326b72215"}
